[WAVE 38 — ServerEntityEventManager (server 579) + NetEntityEventManager.Read]
**NEW — ServerEntityEventManager.Read has no bounds/consistency checks on client event lengths
(server ServerEntityEventManager.cs:475-542):**
- Client sends EntityState: `ushort firstEventID`, `byte eventCount`, then per event: `ushort entityID`.
- If the event ID is stale/out-of-order (line 497-503), server does `msg.BitPosition += msgLength * 8`
  with `msgLength = msg.ReadVariableUInt32()` **unchecked** (up to 2^32-1, and *8 can overflow int).
- If `entity == null` (line 505-518) it does the same unchecked skip.
- For a fresh event (line 526-530), the server reads `msg.ReadBytes(msgLength - 2)` directly — `msgLength`
  is client-controlled and not capped against the remaining packet length; a short packet throws
  out-of-bounds read.
- These reads are NOT wrapped in per-event try/catch (only `ReadEvent` in Update is), so exceptions escape
  to the peer outer catch -> remaining messages in the tick are dropped. Reachable by any in-game client via
  `ClientNetSegment.EntityState`.
- `BufferEvent` caps the buffered list at 512 (drops 256 when exceeded) — good, but only after parsing.
**CORRECTION to Waves 26-29**: ServerEntityEventManager.Update (159-204) wraps each `ReadEvent` in its own
try/catch, so malformed *component* events (CircuitBox `Options[selectedOption]`, ConnectionPanel, etc.)
that get as far as `ReadEvent` are logged (GA error) and dropped **per-event**, not only by the peer outer
catch. The stronger DoS vectors are reads that fail BEFORE the buffer (this `Read` method) and the command/
chat/settings handlers not covered by this per-event catch.
MARKED: server/ServerEntityEventManager.cs 1-579.
[WAVE 39 — Shared ServerSettings ReadPerks allocation DoS + AllowImmediateItemDelivery default]
**NEW — ServerSettings.ReadPerks (shared ServerSettings.cs:1436-1474) unbounded array allocation:**
- `uint coalitionCount = msg.ReadVariableUInt32(); Identifier[] newCoalitionPerks = new Identifier[coalitionCount];`
  (same for `separatistsCount`). No cap. A client can send SERVER_SETTINGS_PERKS with a huge count
  (e.g. 0x0FFFFFFF) -> `new Identifier[huge]` -> massive memory pressure / OutOfMemoryException.
  Reached via server ServerSettings.ReadPerks, whose `HasPermissionToChangePerks` only requires
  ManageSettings OR that no other connected client has ManageSettings — so on a server with no manager
  currently connected (or in PvP with no same-team manager) any client can trigger it.
- Unknown perk IDs are `continue`d, leaving default `Identifier`s in the array (array is replaced, not
  cleared) — minor state corruption only.
**CONFIRMED — AllowImmediateItemDelivery defaults to TRUE (shared ServerSettings.cs:813-818):**
`[Serialize(true,...)] public bool AllowImmediateItemDelivery`. `CampaignMode.AllowImmediateItemDelivery`
returns true by default for any client (unless the setting is explicitly off). So by default a client at
the store can buy items and have them delivered immediately; the `CargoManager.PurchaseItems` path is the
real gate (stock clamp + TryPurchase).
**Shared Client.ReadPermissions (Client.cs:234-266)**: only invoked by ManagePermissions holder;
`ReadRangedInteger(0, (int)ClientPermissions.All)` bounds the permission value, and permitted commands are
matched against existing DebugConsole commands. The catch around int->enum cast is a no-op.
MARKED: shared ServerSettings.cs 500-530/800-825/1436-1520; shared Client.cs 234-266; ReadyCheck server+shared;
shared OrderChatMessage.cs 1-228.
[WAVE 40 — GameServer dispatch + MultiPlayerCampaign ServerRead/ServerReadMoney/ServerReadCrew + FileSender]
**GAMESERVER.READDATAMESSAGE (server GameServer.cs 824-1039) — full header dispatch read:**
- `REQUEST_BACKUP_INDICES` (case at 1032-1033) → `SendBackupIndices` (1041-1051) has **NO PERMISSION CHECK**:
  `string savePath = inc.ReadString(); SaveUtil.GetIndexData(savePath);` then sends the parsed
  `BackupIndexData[]` back to the requesting client. `GetIndexData` (SaveUtil.cs 943-1021) does
  `Path.GetDirectoryName(fullPath)` + `Directory.GetFiles(path, ".{baseName}*")` + `DecompressFile...`.
  Because `path`/`baseName` are attacker-chosen, ANY connected client can: (a) scan arbitrary server
  directories for files matching `.{name}*` backup pattern (existence oracle), (b) force the server to
  decompress arbitrary existing `.xml`/backup files and parse them, returning metadata (location, level
  type, save time). Repeats cause disk I/O / decompression work — cheap client, expensive server.
  Reachable via ClientPacketHeader.REQUEST_BACKUP_INDICES, no ManageSettings/owner requirement.
- `CAMPAIGN_SETUP_INFO` (889-961): new-campaign path reads saveName/seed/subName/subHash and
  `CampaignSettings settings` then calls `Meta... StartNewCampaign` only if `AllowedToManageCampaign(..., ManageRound)`.
  Load path similarly requires ManageRound. OK.
- `FILE_REQUEST` (1020-1024): gated only on `ServerSettings.AllowFileTransfers`, then `FileSender.ReadFileRequest`.
  Types: Submarine (restricted to saved sub matching name+hash), CampaignSave (any client gets the
  **whole current campaign save file** — expected client sync but also full information disclosure sent
  to any connected client; no permission check), Mod (requires `AllowModDownloads` and `ModSender ready`,
  restricted by hash match). `FileTransferOut` reads the **entire file into RAM** (`File.ReadAllBytes`) and
  each transfer is up to 5 per recipient / 16 total; a non-admin can repeatedly request CampaignSave to
  force full-file read+broadcast bandwidth.
- `SERVER_COMMAND` (983-984) → `ClientReadServerCommand`: per-command permission checks; see prior findings.
- `EVENTMANAGER_RESPONSE` (1026-1027) → `GameSession.EventManager.ServerRead(inc, client)` — no auth check.
- `UPDATE_LOBBY`/`UPDATE_INGAME` route through SegmentTableReader; `EntityState` → `entityEventManager.Read`
  (the Wave-38 DoS), `Vote` → `Voting.ServerRead`.
- `READY_CHECK` (1008-1009) → `ReadyCheck.ServerRead` — any client can start/update a 30s ready check,
  no permission/cooldown; moderate (spams all clients with reliable packets + blocks start state).
- `READY_TO_SPAWN` (1559-1567): `SpectateOnly = inc.ReadBoolean() && (AllowSpectating || owner)` — safe.
- `TAKEOVERBOT` (1569-1634): ironman + hireable gate; existing-bot branch requires `AllowBotTakeoverOnPermadeath`.
- `RESPONSE_STARTGAME` (843-855): sets ReadyToStart/AFK — any client can set AFK, only cosmetic.
- `ERROR` (1053-1150): client error report → KickClient on malformed data. Reachable; bounded by math.Min(subCount,5).
**MultiPlayerCampaign.ServerRead (server 835-1158) — sync/validation gaps confirmed:**
- Reads `selectedMissionCount` (byte) then appends `msg.ReadByte()` per mission, `Map.SelectMission`.
- Buy-crate quantities clamped to `availableQuantity - alreadyPurchasedQuantity` and `MathHelper.Clamp(0,...)`,
  then `PurchaseItems`; sell path gated by `SellSubItems`/`SellInventoryItems` permissions (1092-1123).
- Upgrade `purchasedUpgradeCount` (ushort) unlimited loop, each byte level; `UpgradePrefab.Find` can return
  null for arbitrary identifiers (cost/effect guarded in UpgradeManager).
- `purchasedItemSwapCount` (ushort) with `itemToRemoveID`/`itemToInstallIdentifier`; no check that itemToRemove
  is controlled/sold — `UpgradeManager.PurchaseItemSwap` handles affordability; worth re-audit later.
- `Map.SetLocation(currentLocIndex)` if `AllowDebugTeleport` only; `Map.SelectLocation` gated by ManageMap.
- **No top-level `AllowRemoteCampaignInteractions == false` check on the entire `ServerRead`** — every store
  purchase path is wrapped in `HasCampaignInteractionAvailable(sender, InteractionType.Store)`, and upgrades
  in `InteractionType.Upgrade` (1161-1175). So the permission fallback applies when `AllowRemoteCampaignInteractions`
  is on (interactions allowed from anywhere) — but that is an explicit server setting.
**MultiPlayerCampaign.TryPurchase (1507-1537) confirmed prior note**: `if (price == 0) return true`.
`GetBalance` uses wallet+Bank only if `AllowedToManageWallets`.
**ReadyCheck server (readycheck.cs 1-120)** confirmed: `StartReadyCheck` runs with NO permission check,
NO author requirement, no cooldown; reads/updates map via `ActiveReadyCheck`. Repeatable DoS-lite.
**FileSender** (1-438): `ReadFileRequest` CampaignSave branch has no auth; Mod branch gated by AllowModDownloads.
`FileTransferOut` reads entire file to RAM; per-recipient transfer cap = 5, global = 16.
MARKED: server GameServer.cs 1-5010 (already), MultiPlayerCampaign server 1-1702, shared MultiPlayerCampaign 1-357,
SaveUtil.cs 699-1021, FileSender server 1-438, ReadyCheck server+shared, GameServer dispatch ranges.
[WAVE 41 — GameServer.ServerCommand/permission fallback + ReadDataMessage header cartography]
**NEW/CONFIRMED — ClientReadServerCommand permission-gate holes (server GameServer.cs:1720-1995):**
- Top-level check at 1749 (`!sender.HasPermission(command)`) is **skipped entirely for
  `ManageRound` and `ManageCampaign` while a MultiPlayerCampaign is active** (1741-1748):
  `if (command==ManageRound && mpCampaign!=null) {} else if (command==ManageCampaign && mpCampaign!=null) {}
   else if (!sender.HasPermission(command)) return;`
  So ANY in-game/new client can reach the ManageRound/ManageCampaign switch cases, which then rely on
  *ad-hoc* `AllowedToManageCampaign` checks inside the case:
  - `ManageRound` END (1811-1848): gated by `AllowedToManageCampaign(sender, ManageRound)` (would be
    bypassed by the fallback below).
  - `ManageRound` START (1854-1881): the `else if (!GameStarted && !initiatedStartGame)` branch calls
    `TryStartGame()` **with NO permission check at all**. A client with no ManageRound permission can start
    a round (spam: `initiatedStartGame` may be reset each round) — round-start DoS / unprivileged control.
  - `ManageCampaign` (1954-1955): `mpCampaign?.ServerRead(inc, sender)` — the whole campaign-sync read path
    is reached WITHOUT any permission check; internal `AllowedToManageCampaign` gates are the only safety.
- **SelectMode OOB (1945-1953)**: `UInt16 modeIndex = inc.ReadUInt16(); SelectedModeIndex = modeIndex;
  GameModes[modeIndex]` — `GameModes` list index by raw client u16; if `modeIndex >= GameModes.Count`,
  `IndexOutOfRangeException` before any bounds check. Requires `SelectMode` permission only.
- SelectSub (1920-1943) and ManagePermissions/Unban/ConsoleCommands are properly gated by the top-level
  `HasPermission` check.
**NEW/CONFIRMED — CampaignMode.AnyOneAllowedToManageCampaign (shared CampaignMode.cs:197-231) huge fallback:**
- single connected client (Count==1) → true;
- GameStarted && no one has the permission → true ("no-one with permissions, allow anyone to manage");
- GameStarted && someone has permission but round > 60s and that holder is dead/not InGame → true;
- not GameStarted && no one has permission → true.
- This means `AllowedToManageCampaign` (and thus `AllowedToManageWallets`, hire/money/map/reward paths,
  MultiPlayerCampaign.ServerReadMoney 1195, `ServerReadRewardDistribution` 1239, `ResetSalaries` 1255, store
  allowance paths) can return true for a **random client** when: server has 1 client, or no admin/money-holder
  is present/alive, or (once a round goes past 60s) the admin is dead/spectating. Combined with the
  SERVER_COMMAND gate skip, this is the umbrella behind several "no admin ever set permissions"
  privilege escalations.
**MultiPlayerCampaign.ServerRead** (already above) confirmed to run under `ManageCampaign` via
ClientReadServerCommand **without** the HasPermission gate (the inner gates are the only check).
**GameServer.ReadDataMessage header cartography (824-1039):**
- `CAMPAIGN_SETUP_INFO` 889-961: gated by `AllowedToManageCampaign(ManageRound)` (which is expanded by the
  fallback above) for both new and load; `dosProtection.Pause` around StartNewCampaign/LoadCampaign.
- `VOICE` 962-975: voice only if `ServerSettings.VoiceChatEnabled && !Muted`, and sessionId must match.
- `SERVER_SETTINGS`/`SERVER_SETTINGS_PERKS` → ServerSettings.ServerRead / ReadPerks (Wave 37 + 39 findings).
- `ENDROUND_SELF` 986-988: client sets its own InGame=false/ResetSync — self-only, fine.
- `CREW`/`TRANSFER_MONEY`/`REWARD_DISTRIBUTION`/`RESET_REWARD_DISTRIBUTION`/`MEDICAL` — reachable any time,
  resolved in MultiPlayerCampaign (notes above).
- `FILE_REQUEST` 1020: gated on `AllowFileTransfers` (default?) — see FileSender (Wave 40).
- `EVENTMANAGER_RESPONSE` 1026: no auth — single Response read.
- `UPDATE_LOBBY`/`UPDATE_INGAME` include per-segment `SyncIds/ChatMessage/CharacterInput/EntityState/Vote/...`.
- `PING_RESPONSE` 833-841: len checked against `lastPingData`.
- `RESPONSE_CANCEL_STARTGAME` 857-867: any client can abort a round-start warning if active (no permission).
MARKED: CampaignMode server 1-52, shared CampaignMode 185-230/271-288; GameServer 1720-1995.
[WAVE 42 — PrefabCollection indexer DoS confirmed in store read path (unknown identifiers) + CargoManager economics]
**STRONG NEW/CONFIRMED — unknown prefab = server exception in multi-player store path (unprivileged):**
- `PurchasedItem.ItemPrefab` (shared CargoManager.cs:21) is `ItemPrefab.Prefabs[ItemPrefabIdentifier]`.
- `PrefabCollection<T>.this[Identifier]` (PrefabCollection.cs:266-278) does `var prefab = prefabs[identifier].ActivePrefab;`
  — for a **missing identifier** this throws `KeyNotFoundException` (from ConcurrentDictionary indexer)
  BEFORE the `IndexOutOfRangeException` check. So any place that reads `.ItemPrefab` on a client-supplied
  `PurchasedItem`/`SoldItem` with a bogus identifier throws.
- Reachable in `MultiPlayerCampaign.ServerRead`:
  - `ReadSoldItems` (shared MultiPlayerCampaign.cs:351) — parses `ItemPrefab.Prefabs[prefabId]` **at the very top
    of ServerRead (line 854)**, BEFORE ANY interaction/permission check. A client only needs to send
    SERVER_COMMAND:ManageCampaign with a sold-item list containing one unknown prefab id.
  - `ReadPurchasedItems` (294-315) does NOT resolve prefab at parse time (`new PurchasedItem(itemId,...)`),
    but `ServerRead` loops dereference `item.ItemPrefab` (line 973/1035) and `PurchaseItems` (line 312/
    `buyValues[item.ItemPrefab]`) → same exception, though inside the `HasCampaignInteractionAvailable(Store)` gate.
- Because `ClientReadServerCommand` skips the `HasPermission` gate for ManageCampaign while a campaign is active
  (Wave 41), and the exception escapes to `LidgrenServerPeer.Update`'s outer catch (aborts the rest of the
  incoming-message batch), this is a **trivial unprivileged DoS** for any campaign server. Same class as the
  earlier `PrefabCollection` indexer DoS, now with a concrete reachable packet path.
- Fix hint: use `ItemPrefab.Prefabs.TryGet(...)` everywhere a client-supplied id is resolved.
**CargoManager sell/money rules (server path) — verified, no duplicate sale:**
- `SellItems` (server CargoManager 28-66): `sellValues` computed for all non-null prefabs; only
  `store.Balance >= value` and `!item.Removed` gates. For Submarine origin with NullEntityID it picks the first
  matching sellable item from the sub (`GetSellableItemsFromSub` → `IsItemSellable` bounds by prefab/condition/
  container rules). It does NOT require the item to belong to the client — `SellInventoryItems` lets a client
  sell sub items by ID/pack selection; that's the intended system though the permission `SellInventoryItems`
  plus `AllowRemoteCampaignInteractions` means a client can sell sub equipment from anywhere.
- `BuyBackSoldItems` (11-26): uses `campaign.TryPurchase(client, itemValue)` — price==0 returns true (Wave 20).
- `PurchaseItems` (299-377): price = `item.Quantity * buyValues[item.ItemPrefab]`; `TryPurchase` per item.
- **`store.Balance += itemValue`/`-= itemValue` both unbounded** — a high net worth can never be replicated,
  but store balance is used as an upper bound only; no negative-balance issue because the checks are
  `store.Balance < itemValue`.
- `UndeterminedSoldEntities` (426+) is cleared per `GetSellableItemsFromSub` call and is a client-side helper.
**GameServer.LidgrenServerPeer.Update outer catch (135-144)**: ANY exception from any data-message handler
(including all ServerRead* and SERVER_COMMAND paths) also **aborts processing the remaining incoming messages in
that batch** (the foreach is inside the try). So every exception-based DoS above also increases desync/latency,
not just a single player's kick.
MARKED: shared CargoManager 1-520 (ranges accumulate), shared MultiPlayerCampaign 294-355, shared
PrefabCollection 266-317, server CargoManager 1-88, LidgrenServerPeer 104-300, ItemPrefab 510-660 (partial file).
[WAVE 43 — MultiPlayerCampaign.ServerRead store path DoS (unknown store key) + sell/inventory follow-ups]
**NEW STRONG — `purchasedItems` store-id KeyNotFoundException DoS (server MultiPlayerCampaign.cs:1023-1039):**
- Client-controlled `ReadPurchasedItems` returns `Dictionary<Identifier, List<PurchasedItem>>` keyed by an
  **arbitrary store identifier** (no validation that it exists). In ServerRead's `HasCampaignInteractionAvailable(Store)`
  block:
  - buy-crate path (972) checks `map.CurrentLocation.Stores.ContainsKey(store.Key)` (safe);
  - **purchasedItems path (1035) does `map.CurrentLocation.Stores[storeId].Stock.Find(...)` WITHOUT ContainsKey** —
    a nonexistent storeId → `KeyNotFoundException`. Exception escapes to Lidgren batch catch, aborting the rest
    of the tick's messages.
- Also, dereferencing `purchasedItem.ItemPrefab` (1031/1035) throws for **unknown prefab id** (Wave 42), and this
  block is reachable merely by being near a store (or `AllowRemoteCampaignInteractions=true`).
- All reachable from `SERVER_COMMAND:ManageCampaign` path that skips HasPermission for campaign servers.
  Combined: this is the clearest unprivileged DoS in the store/menu flow.
**Inventory.ServerEventRead (server Inventory.cs 1-298) verified — still safe from the "any item" concern:**
- `IsInventoryAccessible()` = `sender.Character.CanAccessInventory(this, AllowFriendly/BotsAndPets)`; gated by
  drag-and-drop setting. Item removals only if `item.IsInteractable(sender.Character)` and not itemAccessDenied;
  item additions need Pickable + not attached + allowed slots + `CanClientAccess(sender)` (191-213). HandLocker
  items require AllowBotsAndPets. So the earlier conclusion stands: Inventory is properly gated, no cross-owner
  item duplication here.
- `SharedRead` slot range `start`/`end` bytes are unchecked (any `end > capacity` → `receivedItemIds[i]` index
  out of range if end > capacity) — but capacity is fixed small; still a malformed-packet exception DoS.
- `itemAccessibility` dict indexed at 124; if an item was removed and not in prevItems but receivedId list has it
  with access denied, the dict lookup could miss (they add prevItems + received items to dictionary, so safe).
**ServerSettings.ServerRead (server 167-265) re-confirmed:**
- Unknown NetProperty key skip: `UInt32 size = incMsg.ReadVariableUInt32(); incMsg.BitPosition += 8*size` —
  unchecked huge/negative size can corrupt seek position; only reachable by ManageSettings holder (admin-level).
MARKED: server MultiPlayerCampaign 1019-1039; server Inventory 1-298; shared Inventory 1108-1176.
[WAVE 44 — ChatMessage Order DoS via unknown order prefab + Order prefab access]
**NEW STRONG — ChatMessage.ServerRead Order crash (unprivileged, in-game):**
- `ChatMessage.ServerRead` (server ChatMessage.cs:19-143) calls `OrderChatMessage.ReadOrder(msg)`
  for `ChatMessageType.Order`. `ReadOrder` (shared OrderChatMessage.cs:154-226) line 168 does
  `var orderPrefab = OrderPrefab.Prefabs[orderIdentifier];` for **any client-supplied identifier**.
- `OrderPrefab.Prefabs` is `PrefabCollection<OrderPrefab>` (Order.cs:41), and `PrefabCollection.this[Identifier]`
  (PrefabCollection.cs:266) throws `KeyNotFoundException` for a missing identifier. There is no `TryGet`.
- Server `ChatMessage.ServerRead` also re-dereferences `OrderPrefab.Prefabs[orderMessageInfo.OrderIdentifier]`
  at line 50. So a single `UPDATE_INGAME` ChatMessage segment with `type=Order` and any bogus order identifier
  (e.g. `"notarealorder"`) throws on the server; the exception is only caught by LidgrenServerPeer.Update's
  outer batch catch (not the `NetStructReadException` per-message catch, which comes from the reader plumbing).
  Result: remaining incoming messages in that tick are dropped, repeated packets = desync/latency DoS.
- No permission or ready-state requirement: any in-game client can send it.
- Same family as Waves 42–43 (missing prefab → exception). Fix: `OrderPrefab.Prefabs.TryGet` before use.
MARKED: shared Order.cs 39-60, shared OrderChatMessage.cs 154-226, server ChatMessage.cs 19-143, shared

[WAVE 45 — EventManager.ServerRead conversation option OOB (unprivileged in-game DoS)]
**NEW STRONG — EventManager.ServerRead `convAction.Options[selectedOption]` out-of-range (server EventManager.cs:28-102):**
- Reads `UInt16 actionId`, `byte selectedOption` directly from client (no bounds check).
- `isIgnore = selectedOption == byte.MaxValue`; otherwise needs to be a valid option index.
- At line 80 the only guard is: `convAction.Options.Any() && !convAction.GetEndingOptions().Contains(selectedOption)`.
  `GetEndingOptions()` (shared ConversationAction.cs:307-322) returns **only valid option indices** (and -1).
  There is **no `selectedOption < Options.Count` check**. If a client that is a `TargetClient` of a
  `ConversationAction` sends `selectedOption` larger than `Options.Length`, line 82 `var option =
  convAction.Options[selectedOption];` → `ArgumentOutOfRangeException`.
- Reachable via `ClientPacketHeader.EVENTMANAGER_RESPONSE` → `EventManager.ServerRead` (GameServer.cs:1026-1027),
  no auth/require-being-target beyond `convAction.TargetClients.Contains(sender)` (line 44). Non-targets are
  silently `IgnoreClient`'d. So only the intended conversation participant can trigger it, but that is still
  any player in a scripted event conversation.
- Exception escapes to the outer batch catch (LidgrenServerPeer.Update 135-144), dropping the rest of the
  tick's incoming messages (on top of `ClientReadServerCommand` ManageCampaign path uses the same catch).
- **Same class as Waves 42–44: client-controlled index into a dynamic list without a lower/upper bound.**
  Fix pattern: `if (selectedOption < 0 || selectedOption >= convAction.Options.Count) { ignore }`.
MARKED: shared ConversationAction 238-322; server EventManager 28-102; server GameServer 1020-1051(already).
[WAVE 46 — UpgradeManager purchase/swap: permission fallback + null itemToInstall NRE + arbitrary swappable target]
**UpgradeManager (shared UpgradeManager.cs 172-470) findings:**
- `TryPurchaseUpgrade` (172) & `PurchaseItemSwap` (304) & `CancelItemSwap` (409) all gate on
  `HasPermissionToManageUpgrades(client)` = `CampaignMode.AllowedToManageCampaign(client, ManageCampaign)` —
  inherits the Wave-41 fallback (anyone when no admin / single client / past 60s with admin dead).
- `PurchaseItemSwap` line 306-345: itemToRemove comes from `Entity.FindEntityByID(itemToRemoveID)` (client
  controls ID) in `MultiPlayerCampaign.ServerRead` line 879; **no check that the item is at the upgrade area
  or owned by the client's team/sub**, only `AllowSwapping` + `!HiddenInGame` + category-match + prefab is
  Swappable. So a client with the (often-fallback) ManageCampaign permission can swap/queue-swap ANY
  swappable item in the level, paying the price (not isNetworkMessage here).
- **`PurchaseItemSwap` line 328 NRE**: if client sends `itemToInstallIdentifier = Empty` (line 878: `null`)
  and the removed item is in a matching upgrade category, `itemToInstall.Tags` → NullReferenceException.
  Reached from `MultiPlayerCampaign.ServerRead` with client-supplied IDs; escapes to outer batch catch.
- `TryPurchaseUpgrade` price can be negative (only logged, not clamped); `price < 0` still proceeds to
  `Campaign.TryPurchase(client, price)` → negative price accepted by `TryPurchase` (balance + negative = free
  money? Actually `TryDeduct(negative)` likely adds). But `prefab.Price` is content-controlled, not packet.
  Not an exploit unless modded content has negative prices.
**Verified CANCEL path**: server reads `purchasedItemSwapCount` (ushort) unsized loop; each entry at most
`itemToRemoveID` + `itemToInstallIdentifier` — lightweight; `UpgradeManager.PurchaseItemSwap`/`CancelItemSwap`
dominate.
MARKED: shared UpgradeManager 160-470.
[WAVE 47 — Campaign.ServerRead remote-repair / debug-teleport gaps + ServerReadMoney sink]
**Confirmed in MultiPlayerCampaign.ServerRead (server):**
- Hull repairs / device repairs / lost-shuttle retrieval (lines 908-939) run BEFORE the
  `HasCampaignInteractionAvailable(Store)` guard and are only money gated. Because
  `ClientReadServerCommand` allows any client to reach `ServerRead` in campaign (ManageCampaign skip,
  Wave 41), a client can trigger them from anywhere (not at the outpost/upgrade NPC). Cost is charged to
  sender (or bank if wallet-manage fallback), so it is not free money — but it bypasses the "remote
  interactions" setting for at minimum the repair/shuttle purchases.
- `currentLocIndex` (line 941-944): `if (currentLocIndex < Map.Locations.Count && Map.AllowDebugTeleport)
  Map.SetLocation(currentLocIndex);` — gated ONLY by `AllowDebugTeleport`, NOT by `ManageMap`/`ManageCampaign`.
  If debug teleport is enabled, ANY client can move the campaign location (e.g. teleport ship to an arbitrary
  index) even without any campaign permission. This is a genuine authorization gap (debug flag only).
- `selectedLocIndex`/`selectedMissionIndices` are properly gated by `AllowedToManageCampaign(ManageMap)` (946-952).
**MultiPlayerCampaign.ServerReadMoney (1177-1233) — money-destruction sink:**
- `TransferMoney(from)` at 1209-1226: `if (!from.TryDeduct(amount)) return;` then if receiver is an
  **invalid/unresolvable** wallet (1213-1217) it returns AFTER `TryDeduct`. So a wallet-managing client can
  send `NetWalletTransfer` with a valid sender and a receiver CharacterID that doesn't exist → the amount is
  **deducted and permanently destroyed** (not credited to the bank or anyone). `GetWalletByID` returns
  `Wallet.Invalid` for a nonexistent `ushort id`; no compensate path. Not a money-printer (it only destroys),
  but it lets an admin/fallback-offender erase arbitrary wallet balances. Combined with the Wave 41 fallback
  (AllowedToManageWallets true for any client when no manager active), this is an **unprivileged money
  destruction** option on servers without an active money-holder at >60s round.
**Campaign.TryHireCharacter (shared CampaignMode.cs 1137-1167)**: `takeMoney && TryPurchase(client, price)` —
price is `NewCharacterCost` or salary; `price==0 => true` only if cost is literally 0. No exploit beyond prior.
MARKED: shared CampaignMode 1100-1170; server MultiPlayerCampaign 835-1233 (accumulated).
[WAVE 48 — SaveUtil backup-index path traversal/parse + ServerSettings unbounded reads + Character input OOB]
**NEW HIGH — REQUEST_BACKUP_INDICES → SaveUtil.GetIndexData is an unprivileged arbitrary path scan/oracle + decompression DoS:**
- Server `GameServer.SendBackupIndices` (1041-1051) reads `savePath` from the client (ANY connected client)
  then `SaveUtil.GetIndexData(savePath)` and replies with the parsed backup list.
- `SaveUtil.GetIndexData` (943-1021) computes `path = Path.GetDirectoryName(fullPath)` and
  `fileName = Path.GetFileNameWithoutExtension(fullPath)` from the CLIENT STRING, then
  `Directory.GetFiles(path, $".{fileName}{BackupExtension}*", ...)` and for every match calls
  `ExtractGameSessionRootElementFromSaveFile(backupPath)` (722-760) which **decompresses the file
  (DecompressFileToStream) and byte-scans it** before returning location/level/save-time to the attacker.
- Consequences (unprivileged, no ManageSettings/Ban):
  1. Arbitrary directory enumeration/existence oracle: server will list files matching `.{name}.bak*`
     (and similar) in any path the server process can read, e.g. relative traversal / server config dirs,
     returning metadata for files that parse as Barotrauma saves.
  2. Server-side decompression + XML parse of attacker-path files = CPU/IO DoS; repeated requests amplify.
  3. Information disclosure of campaign backup metadata (location names, level type, save time) to any client.
- Only mitigation: the `FileName` search mask is prefixed with `.`, so files must match the backup naming
  convention, and `GetBackupPaths` catches exceptions (returns empty), but the scan/decompress happens anyway.
**ServerSettings.ReadExtraCargo (1368-1389)** — `count = msg.ReadUInt32(); for (i < count)` loops on a
client-controlled count with no upper bound; caps apply only AFTER each new item is found, so a malformed
packet with huge `count` either runs long (each iter reads a string) or throws OOB. ManageSettings-gated,
but the same "unchecked VUVarUInt/count" pattern as ReadPerks (Wave 39).
**Character.ServerReadInput (server CharacterNetworking.cs 189-267):**
- `inputCount = msg.ReadByte()` and the loop reads `ReadRangedInteger` + `ReadUInt16()` (+ more for Use/Grab)
  per input. No check that the remaining message actually contains `inputCount` records; a short packet with
  a large `inputCount` causes out-of-bounds read (exception → outer batch catch). Same `Character.ServerEventRead`
  `UpdateTalents` `talentCount = msg.ReadUInt16()` (313) loop `ReadUInt32` per talent is also unbounded and can
  OOB short packets. Reached only by the client controlling the character (not privileged).
- Note: `memInput` is capped at 60 (261-266), so buffering is bounded; the DoS is the OOB-read exception, not memory.
MARKED: SaveUtil 699-1021 (already partial), ServerSettings 1354-1476, CharacterNetworking 189-348.
[WAVE 49 — Item ServerEventRead ChangeProperty: unbounded string[] allocation (unprivileged OOM DoS)]
**NEW HIGH — `Item.ReadPropertyChange` string[] element: client-controlled allocation → server OOM:**
- Entry: `Item.ServerEventRead` (server Items/Item.cs 190-242) `EventType.ChangeProperty` → `ReadPropertyChange` (shared Item.cs 3855-4035) `else if (type == typeof(string[]))` at 3981-3993.
- `int arrayLength = msg.ReadInt32(); string[] val = new string[arrayLength];` — `arrayLength` is a **client-controlled int** with **no clamp** (unlike the `Editable.MaxLength` clamp used for single strings at 3910-3914).
- The only gate before allocation is `allowEditing` = `CanClientAccess(sender)`/circuit-box-unlocked + `ConditionallyEditable.IsEditable`. For any `[Editable]`/`[InGameEditable]` string[] property on an item the client can access (common item), sending `int.MaxValue` → OOM on the server; even modest huge values cause allocation churn.
- Also there is **no per-entry MaxLength** clamp for string elements (3906-3914 applies to single string only; `val[i] = msg.ReadString()` at 3987 is raw), so huge per-element strings can be set on accessible items and then **logged** at 4031 (log spam / disk growth).
- Reached by ANY client (not a permission). The outer batch catch in LidgrenServerPeer will catch the OOM? An `OutOfMemoryException` is not reliably catchable; even if caught, the allocation itself may destabilize the process. This is the strongest "any client can kill the server" item so far.
- Fix hints: clamp `arrayLength` against `EditableAttribute.MaxLength` (or a serialized safe cap, e.g. `0..64`) before `new string[...]`; clamp each `val[i]` length; cap log string.
**Other paths in the same switch (verified safe-ish):**
- `ComponentState`/`InventoryState` indices use `ReadRangedInteger(0, components.Count-1)` → safe bounds.
- `Treatment`: must `CanInteractWith(this)`, `targetCharacter != c.Character && c.Character.SelectedCharacter != targetCharacter` guard, healing cooldown → no cross-target healing.
- `Combine`: must `CanInteractWith` both this and combineTarget → no remote combine.
MARKED: shared Item 3855-4035, server Item 190-242.
[WAVE 50 — P2P MessageDefragmenter unprivileged memory exhaustion + fragment count OOB]
**NEW HIGH — Shared MessageDefragmenter.ProcessIncomingFragment (lines 11-39):**
- Entry: P2P server `P2PServerPeer.HandleDataMessage` (200-210) reads `MessageFragment` from an already-connected
  client (`IsDataFragment` branch) and feeds it to `connectedClient.Defragmenter.ProcessIncomingFragment`.
- `FragmentId` = `ushort FragmentIndex, ushort FragmentCount, ushort MessageId` — all client-supplied.
- Line 15: `partialMessages[fragment.FragmentId.MessageId] = new MessageFragment[fragment.FragmentId.FragmentCount];`
  → allocates an array of up to 65k entries per **new unique MessageId**, and `partialMessages` is never evicted
  (only removed when a *complete* message arrives, line 35).
- No cap on `partialMessages.Count`, no timeout, no fragment-count sanity check relative to real MTU/usage.
- Exploit: an authenticated but **unprivileged** client sends e.g. thousands of `IsDataFragment` packets, each
  with a distinct `MessageId` and `FragmentCount=0xFFFF` and one fragment. Server allocates and retains a
  ~65k-element array per message → server memory exhaustion (a few thousand packets → GBs). On Steam/EOS P2P
  relayed servers this is reachable from the wild. Lidgren (dedicated IP) servers don't use this path, but
  the shared code is hit by any `ChildServerRelay`/P2P game.
- Secondary: line 25 `fragment.FragmentIndex >= fragmentBuffer.Length` correctly bounds the *index*, but the
  `FragmentCount` itself is used as allocation size before any check. Also line 36 joins all fragment `Data`
  arrays; a full 65k-fragment message is ~65k * 1100 bytes ≈ 72 MB single allocation plus copy.
- Fix hints: cap `FragmentCount` (e.g. <= 256 / real max fragments from MTU), limit `partialMessages` count &
  add per-message timeout, and reject first-fragment counts that don't match a plausible size.
**P2PServerPeer.Send/paths verified (lines 307-423)**: sends only to connected/owner; no exploit.
**ChildServerRelay (shared 1-368)**: pipe frame `msgLength` is 4-byte so max ~4GB, but `Write` rejects
`>0x1fff_ffff` and `Read` yields messages per queued byte[]; no new exploit in relay itself.
MARKED: MessageFragmenter, MessageFragment, MessageDefragmenter, server P2PServerPeer, shared ChildServerRelay,
client/server ChildServerRelay partials.
[WAVE 51 — MultiPlayerCampaign full sweep done: ServerReadCrew/reward money]
**MultiPlayerCampaign.ServerReadCrew (1263-1434):**
- `pendingHireLength = msg.ReadUInt16()` → `new ushort[pendingHireLength]` + `new bool[...]` up to 65535
  (bounded-ish; per-message 128KB alloc + read OOB on truncated → exception DoS). Gated by
  `HasCampaignInteractionAvailable(sender, InteractionType.Crew)`.
- `fireCharacter`/`renameCharacter`/`validateHires` all gated by `AllowedToManageCampaign(...)+interaction`;
  self-rename path verified. No new exploit, but reinforces: on a server with the fallback
  (`AllToManageCampaign` true when no active manager), a client needs only to be physically at the crew NPC
  to hire/fire/rename — a permission-model gap, not a bug per se.
- **ServerReadRewardDistribution/Salaries (1235-1261)** gated by `AllowedToManageWallets` — same fallback.
**TryPurchase (1507-1537) confirmed:**
- `if (price == 0) return true;` → any zero-priced purchase (content-controlled, but also any server with
  `StartupPrice=0`-style content) is free. Combined with `GetBalance`/fallback it's the same family.
- If sender is `AllowedToManageWallets`, a purchase can spend from the shared `Bank` for the client's
  shortfall (1528-1533) — that is intended admin/manager behavior but with the Wave-41 fallback it becomes
  "any client can loot the bank" on poorly configured servers.
MARKED: server MultiPlayerCampaign 1-1702 (full).
[WAVE 52 — CargoManager shared 1-840 review (store buy cost + immediate delivery)]
**CargoManager.PurchaseItems (299-377) — store buy path:**
- Checked `ModifyItemQuantityInBuyCrate` (265-280): quantity clamped upstream in ServerRead to
  `availableQuantity - alreadyPurchased`; no negative overflow.
- `PurchaseItems` line 312: `itemValue = item.Quantity * buyValues[item.ItemPrefab]`. If `buyValues` is
  negative for a content/price-modifier that yields negative price, `TryPurchase(client, negative)` can add
  money (`TryDeduct(negative)`). This is content-controlled (store prices), NOT a raw packet exploit, but with
  mods it's the same negative-price family.
- **Immediate delivery to the buyer** (338-361): if `DeliverImmediately` is set (client-supplied bit in
  `PurchasedItem`), the server spawns the purchased items directly into `client.Character` at the store.
  The server does NOT verify the buyer is physically at the store in `PurchaseItems` itself — the outer
  `ServerRead` wraps it in `HasCampaignInteractionAvailable(sender, Store)`, so an outpost-store client is
  fine. Note for modders: calling `PurchaseItems` directly must be gated.
- Buy-crate `availableQuantity` clamp is duplicated/safe when the store exists; the missing `ContainsKey`
  (line 1035 server file, prior wave) still stands for purchased items with a bogus store id.
MARKED: shared CargoManager 1-840.
[WAVE 53 — SegmentTableReader.Read: attacker-controlled tablePointer with no bounds check]
**NEW HIGH — shared SegmentTable.cs 265-324 (shared/used by server and client):**
- `SegmentTableReader<T>.Read` reads `int tablePointer = msg.ReadInt32()` (line 271) directly from the packet,
  then sets `msg.BitPosition = pointerLocation + tablePointer` (272-278) with **no check** that `tablePointer`
  is >=0 and within `msg.LengthBits`/buffer bounds.
- A malicious client can set `tablePointer` to an arbitrary large/negative value in ANY packet that uses
  `SegmentTableWriter` (e.g. `UPDATE_INGAME` / `UPDATE_LOBBY` server-side reads at GameServer:1264,1367).
  Consequences:
  1. `msg.BitPosition` is moved outside the valid message range; the subsequent `numSegments = msg.ReadUInt16()`
     and per-segment `INetSerializableStruct.Read<Segment<T>>` read at arbitrary offsets. Reading within the
     allocated buffer but past `LengthBits` yields stale memory interpreted as `(Identifier, Pointer)` tuples;
     reading past the end throws `IndexOutOfRange`/`ArgumentOutOfRange`.
  2. `numSegments` is a raw `UInt16` → up to 65535 `Segment` allocations (bounded, but combined with huge
     negative `tablePointer` the reads corrupt state and hit the outer `LidgrenServerPeer.Update` catch → the
     whole packet batch aborts → desync/latency DoS).
- The per-segment `SegmentReadMsg` constructor DOES validate `offset+lengthBits >= underlying.LengthBits`
  (151-155), but that check happens only AFTER the table was already read from an attacker-chosen offset.
- Recording: any `SegmentTableReader` consumer should validate `tablePointer >= 0 &&
  pointerLocation + tablePointer < LengthBits` (and `numSegments <= max`) BEFORE jumping.
MARKED: shared SegmentTable 1-330 (full).
[WAVE 54 — FileReceiver client validation + FileSender info-disclosure]
**Client FileReceiver (client side, 1-600):**
- `ValidateInitialData` (458-500) checks `fileSize` against a per-type cap (500 MiB mods, 50 MiB others),
  invalid filename chars, and extension (.sub/.save). This blocks classic path traversal because
  `Path.GetInvalidFileNameCharsCrossPlatform` includes `/`/`\` on Windows; on Linux `\` is not separator so
  `fileName="..\x.sub"` just writes a filename with a backslash (harmless on Linux). Reasonable, keep.
- `TransferOnSameMachine` (324-352): server tells the owner-only client a raw `filePath` and the client calls
  `OnFinished` on it — if a third-party were to spoof `OwnerConnection`, they could point the client at an
  arbitrary existing file path (the client uses `File.Exists` only). Owner-channel only; not a wild exploit.
- Data frames with arbitrary `offset` are buffered with a cap (`DataBufferSize=50`); out-of-order is handled
  safely.
**Server FileSender.ReadFileRequest (lines 336-436) — CAMPAIGN SAVE disclosure:**
- `FileTransferType.CampaignSave` (399-408): **any connected client** (no `ManageSettings`/`ManageCampaign`
  permission check) can request the campaign save file. The only gate is `ServerSettings.AllowFileTransfers`
  at GameServer.cs:1021, which is **default true** (`[Serialize(true,...)]` in ServerSettings.cs:644-649).
- Server responds by starting a transfer of `GameMain.GameSession.DataPath.LoadPath` — the full current
  campaign save, including bank/character data/faction/map state. On default public servers this lets any
  connected player download the entire campaign save (information disclosure and a basis for offline
  duplication/hacking). On a peer-hosted P2P server this goes out over the file-transfer channel too.
- `Submarine` request matches only `SavedSubmarines` (name+hash) — fine.
- `Mod` request is gated by `AllowModDownloads` + `ModSender.Ready` and matched by hash — fine.
- **Recommendation for mods/servers:** disable `AllowFileTransfers` unless sharing subs is actually needed,
  or gate `CampaignSave` behind `AllowedToManageCampaign(ManageCampaign)`.
**Server FileTransfer ack branch (347-377):** a client can set `expecting` arbitrarily → `KnownReceivedOffset
= Math.Max(expecting, ...)` can jump to `Data.Length` and finish the transfer early (ability to cancel/stop
own transfer; small DoS, no server leak). `lastSeen` is `Math.Min(SentOffset, clientVal)` so it can't force
server to skip past what it sent.
MARKED: client FileReceiver 1-600 (full); server FileSender already 1-439.
[WAVE 55 — ReadTakeOverBot: reserve-bench overflow of AllowBotTakeoverOnPermadeath + free-bot fallback]
**GameServer.ReadTakeOverBotMessage (1569-1633):**
- Branch 1608-1618: `botInfo is { Character: null } && (botInfo.IsNewHire || botInfo.IsOnReserveBench)` spawns a
  bot (or queues it in the respawn shuttle) and takes it over WITHOUT checking
  `ServerSettings.AllowBotTakeoverOnPermadeath`. The regular bot branch (1624) checks that flag, but the
  reserve-bench/new-hire branch does not.
- Impact: in Permadeath mode with bot takeover disabled, a dead client can still spawn as a reserve-bench/new
  hire bot by sending `TAKEOVERBOT` with the bot's ID. `SpawnAndTakeOverBotInShuttle` even calls
  `mpCampaign.DiscardClientCharacterData(client)` (1696), removing the permanently-dead character record.
  This is a **permadeath/ironman-adjacent bypass** (ironman is blocked at 1574-1578, so ironman is safe; regular
  permadeath-without-ironman is affected).
- Branch 1582-1584: `ReplaceCostPercentage <= 0 || AllowedToManageCampaign(ManageMoney|ManageHires)` lets a
  client hire + take over an outpost hireable character. With the Wave-41 fallback (no permission holder alive
  >60s) this becomes any-client-anywhere free bot takeover / character replacement.
- NOTE: regular `sender.TryTakeOverBot` (1626) still enforces permadeath + permanently dead internally; the
  gap is only the reserve-bench branch above.
**UpdateCharacterInfo (4361-4420)**: validated — name sanitized, `charInfoRateLimiter`, existing campaign
data cannot be replaced; no exploit.
MARKED: GameServer 1-5010 already; no new file.
[WAVE 56 — SERVER_COMMAND ManageRound permission-skip: unprivileged round start]
**NEW HIGH — GameServer.ClientReadServerCommand (1720-1995):**
- Lines 1741-1748: for `command == ManageRound` or `Management==ManageCampaign`, the generic
  `sender.HasPermission(command)` check is bypassed whenever `GameSession.GameMode is MultiPlayerCampaign`.
  The comment says "checked in more detail below", but the `ManageRound, end=false` path is NOT fully checked:
- Line 1870 (`else if (!GameStarted && !initiatedStartGame)`) calls `TryStartGame()` with **no**
  `AllowedToManageCampaign`/`ManageRound` check. So ANY connected client in a campaign can send
  `SERVER_COMMAND { command=ManageRound(end=false) }` and start the running round, even without any
  permission. On a public campaign server this is an unprivileged round-start (invites/DoS, restarting,
  preventing the host from setting up).
- `ManageRound, end=true` DOES check `AllowedToManageCampaign(sender, ManageRound)` (1815-1817) — but with the
  Wave-41 fallback that returns true when no permission-holder is alive/in-game for >60s.
- `ManageCampaign` command (1954-1955) invokes `mpCampaign.ServerRead` for ANY client. `ServerRead` has inner
  permissions/money gates; but the **hull repair / item repair / lost shuttle purchases (908-939) have NO
  `AllowedToManageCampaign` gate** — only `GetBalance>=cost && TryPurchase`. Impact: any client can spend their
  own wallet (or the shared Bank if they have wallet-manage fallback) on campaign-wide services without
  ManageCampaign. This is an authorization gap, not free money, but it lets unprivileged players mutate
  campaign state (repairs/retrieve shuttle).
- `SelectMode` line 1947-1949: `SelectedModeIndex` is clamped by its setter, but line 1949 indexes
  `GameModes[rawModeIndex]` directly → `IndexOutOfRangeException` with `modeIndex` > length (already noted as
  Wave-41 SelectMode OOB). Reachable by any client with SelectMode permission only (gated above), so it is a
  permission-holder crash; the ManageRun/ManageCampaign skips above are the unprivileged ones.
MARKED: GameServer.cs 1-5010 (already).
[WAVE 57 — RESOLVED: AllowedToManage* definitions + AnyOneAllowedToManageCampaign fallback]
**Definitions resolved:**
- Server `CampaignMode.cs` 18-41 (BarotraumaServer/ServerSource/GameSession/GameModes):
  `AllowedToManageCampaign(client, perms)` = `client.HasPermission(perms) || client.HasPermission(ManageCampaign) || IsOwner(client) || AnyOneAllowedToManageCampaign(perms)`.
  `IsOwner(client)` = `client.Connection == GameMain.Server.OwnerConnection`.
- Client `CampaignMode.cs` 114-129 (BarotraumaClient/ClientSource/GameSession/GameModes): same logic using `GameMain.Client`.
- Shared `CampaignMode.cs` 197-231: `AnyOneAllowedToManageCampaign(perms)`:
  * if NetworkMember null -> true
  * if ConnectedClients.Count == 1 -> true
  * if GameStarted: if SOMEONE has perms -> after RoundDuration >= 60s, returns true when NO perm-holder alive+in-game (dead/incap/left). If no one has perms -> true.
  * else (not started) -> true when no perm-holder/client owner connected.
**NEW MEDIUM/HIGH (by-design admin fallback, demonstrably exploitable on public server):**
- ANY client is allowed to manage the campaign / wallets / hires / map / repairs / shuttle recovery whenever the permission holder is
  dead, incapacitated, or disconnected for >60s (or no permission holder exists on a dedicated server). Consequence on a
  public/community server: attacker can kill the owner (or wait until owner's character dies in-game), then:
  - spend the shared Bank via wallet-manage fallback (line 903 AllowedToManageWallets),
  - change map / buy items / buy repairs, purchase shuttles,
  - hire/take-over/manage crew (GameServer 1583-1584 free hire + takeover branch relies on same fallback),
  - kick/ban/start round via the ManageRound/ManageCampaign command paths.
- `Dedicated` servers with no `OwnerConnection` and no perm-holders: `AnyOneAllowedToManageCampaign` returns true while `ConnectedClients.Count==1` only; with >1 non-perm clients GameStarted false -> None(owner||perm-holder) == true -> true. So a lone first joiner (or by extension any client while no perm-holder in lobby) can manage. This is intentional anti-lockout but has no throttle/authentication beyond permissions.
MARKED: client CampaignMode.cs 1-470; shared CampaignMode.cs 1201-1757 remains for next pass (190 was the 197-231 snippet; the file's rest still unmarked).
[WAVE 58 — Fabricator free-craft nuance + EntityState unbounded length DoS]
**NEW LOW/MEDIUM (refine of user HIT #6) — Fabricator `Fabricate()` ignores `TryPurchase` result:**
- `Fabricator.Fabricate()` (Shared Fabricator.cs 376-396): when `RequiredMoney>0`, in MP campaign it calls
  `mpCampaign.TryPurchase(client, price)` and ignores the return value; in SP it calls `user.Wallet.Deduct` /
  `campaign.Bank.Deduct` without checking. However `CanBeFabricated()` (761-779) calls `mpCampaign.CanAfford(...)`
  BEFORE both `StartFabricating` and `Fabricate`, so a plainly broke player cannot even start. The remaining window is
  a TOCTOU: another purchase/spend between the last `CanBeFabricated` and the `TryPurchase` can cause fabrication to
  complete without payment. Severity: low-medium; not a clean free-craft as claimed.
- **`ServerEventRead` (server Fabricator.cs 9-32)** reads a client-supplied `recipeHash` and indexes
  `fabricationRecipes[recipeHash]` at line 30 without checking `ContainsKey`. Any client that can access a fabricator
  (Item.CanClientAccess) and sends an unknown hash triggers `KeyNotFoundException`. NOT a crash: the event is read via
  `ServerEntityEventManager.Update` which wraps `ReadEvent` in `try/catch(Exception)` (189-201), so it's just
  error/analytics noise; the sender is not kicked.
**NEW MEDIUM/HIGH — ServerEntityEventManager.Read unbounded msgLength (DoS):**
- `ServerEntityEventManager.Read` (475-541) reads `int msgLength = (int)msg.ReadVariableUInt32()` from every client
  entity event, then immediately does `msg.ReadBytes(msgLength - 2)` at line 529. There is NO upper bound check and
  NO minimum (msgLength<2 passes negative to ReadBytes -> array overflow exception; msgLength huge -> huge allocation).
  Path is `ClientReadIngame -> case EntityState -> entityEventManager.Read(inc, c)` (GameServer 1483-1484) with NO
  try/catch around it; the only protection is `LidgrenServerPeer.Update` outer catch (135-144) which catches the
  exception (but an OOM-scale allocation may still kill the process; in any case the peer receives a very large
  allocation request per event). **Any connected in-game client can repeatedly send msgLength=0xFFFFFFFE to pressure
  server memory or trigger an exception flood.** The buffer count guard (BufferEvent, >512) does not help because the
  exception happens before BufferEvent.
- Further: `Read` is also called from `ClientReadIngame` segment reader which is inside `ReadDataMessage`; no
  per-packet `NetStructReadException` catches this (those only wrap `PeerPacketHeaders`/`PeerPacketMessage` in
  `LidgrenServerPeer.HandleDataMessage`).
**LidgrenServerPeer (1-574):** authenticated flow OK; malformed handshake banned by IP (233-243); content-package-order
step (user HIT #10) is on line 386 (pendingClient.InitializationStep set without reading back ack — informational,
reuses the same InitStep enum), no per-file authorization; end-of-`HandleDataMessage` can be abused to make server
read an extra `PeerPacketMessage` after a malformed header? No — `ReadConnectionInitializationStep` handled separately.
MARKED: Fabricator.cs 1-1110; server Fabricator.cs 1-78; ServerEntityEventManager.cs 1-579; LidgrenServerPeer.cs 1-574.
[WAVE 59 — P2P/ServerPeer: ContentPackageOrder bypass + reconnect password skip]
**NEW HIGH (confirmed user HIT #10) — join without required mods:**
- `ServerPeer.ReadConnectionInitializationStep` case `ConnectionInitialization.ContentPackageOrder` (207-210) sets
  `pendingClient.InitializationStep = Success` WITHOUT reading or validating the client's mod list. The server only
  *sends* `ServerPeerContentPackageOrderPacket` (303-313); there is no check that the client acks the package set and
  no `ContentPackageOrder` response read from the client. A client only needs to send a packet whose
  `PacketHeader.IsConnectionInitializationStep` is `ContentPackageOrder` to advance to `Success`. So **a client can
  join a modded server without having the required content packages**; the server will proceed to the lobby with
  mismatched content (desync / missing item events / potential sync breakers). In stock this is blocked only by
  ModSender when `AllowModDownloads` is on (auto-download) or by the client itself; the server never rejects.
- **P2P child-server trust:** `P2PServerPeer.ProcessAuthTicket` (412-421) explicitly does nothing — "assume that
  authentication succeeded". Combined with `P2POwnerToServerHeader` trusting the endpoint forwarded by the owner
  process (128-129), the *owner* (host) process is the trust boundary; if compromise occurs there, full auth bypass.
- `P2PServerPeer.HandleDataMessage` (219-303): any remote sender can send `P2POwnerToServerHeader` with an arbitrary
  endpoint (only the owner process normally fills it), so a malicious owner-side frame is needed; not remotely
  exploitable from a client.
- `ServerPeer.ShouldAskForPassword` (399-409): `FindAndRemoveRecentlyDisconnectedConnection` skips password for
  recently disconnected connections — a reconnect convenience that could be abused if IP/account matching is weak.
MARKED: P2PServerPeer.cs 1-423; ServerPeer.cs 1-411.

[WAVE 61 — component ServerRead sweep: mostly gated; one DoS in ReadPropertyChange]
**Server item components (all `ServerEventRead` gated by `item.CanClientAccess(c)`/`CanInteractWith`):** Holdable 1-55,
Deconstructor 1-26, Engine 1-34, Pump 1-66, Reactor 1-59, Steering 1-135, PowerContainer 1-31, ConnectionPanel 1-216,
WifiComponent 1-35, Wire 1-78, Terminal 1-113, ButtonTerminal 1-20, CustomInterface 1-93. No authorization bypass;

**NEW LOW/MEDIUM DoS — `Item.ReadPropertyChange` string[] allocation (Shared Item.cs 3981-3993):**
- `EventType.ChangeProperty` reaches `ReadPropertyChange(inGameEditableOnly: true, sender: c)` from
  `Item.ServerEventRead` (server Item.cs 230-231). It parses the property identifier, then for a `string[]` property
  does `int arrayLength = msg.ReadInt32(); string[] val = new string[arrayLength];` **before** `allowEditing` is
  checked. A client with CanClientAccess to any item that exposes an in-game-editable `string[]` property can craft an
  arrayLength near `int.MaxValue` to trigger a huge allocation/OutOfMemory (caught by outer
  `ServerEntityEventManager.Update` only if `ReadEvent` runs there, but `Item.ServerEventRead` is invoked there too;
  the catch is `catch(Exception)`). Logging/error spam or process pressure. Niche (needs a `string[]` InGameEditable
  property) but real.
**Item.ServerEventRead index/type errors (server Item.cs 185-242):** `ComponentState`/`InventoryState` use
`ReadRangedInteger(0, components.Count-1)` but then do `(components[componentIndex] as IClientSerializable).ServerEventRead`
without checking the cast result; a component that is not `IClientSerializable` yields NRE caught by the event manager.
Not a crash, error/noise.

[WAVE 62 — remaining item-component server partials: no client-writable path]
- All remaining server item-component partials are IServerSerializable-only (server->client writes) or gate writes on
  `item.CanClientAccess`: Door 1-53 (no ServerEventRead; Door is not IClientSerializable), Scanner 1-14, Rope 1-32,
  Projectile 1-87, LevelResource 1-14, GeneticMaterial 1-20, Growable 1-63, ItemLabel 1-92, LightComponent 1-45,
  Controller 1-13, TriggerComponent 1-12, Repairable 1-59 (gated + non-traitor sabotage forced to Repair),
  PowerDistributor 1-33 (gated), MemoryComponent/ConnectionSelectorComponent (server-write only).
- **CircuitBox.ServerEventRead (1-393):** every mutation opcode gated by `CanAccessAndUnlocked` (IsLocked +
  CanClientAccess + ClientHasRequiredItems); `MoveComponent`/`Select*` only require `item.CanClientAccess` (no resource
  cost). Unknown opcode -> `ArgumentOutOfRangeException` (line 295-296) is caught by `ServerEntityEventManager.Update`
  -> error noise only; not a crash.
MARKED: above files + CircuitBox 1-393.
[WAVE 63 — server-side deserialization + campaign store]
**HIGH — unauthenticated OOM/DoS in ServerEntityEventManager.Read (server EntityEventManager 475-541):**
- Client sends `ClientNetSegment.EntityState`; `Read` reads `msgLength = (int)msg.ReadVariableUInt32()` (line 492) with
  NO upper bound and then `byte[] temp = msg.ReadBytes(msgLength - 2)` (line 529) which allocates
  `new byte[msgLength-2]` BEFORE it validates that the remaining buffer is large enough
  (MsgReader.ReadBytes, Message.cs:427-433). A client can put e.g. 5 bytes `FF FF FF FF 0F` in an EntityState
  segment to request ~268 MB (or up to ~1-2 GB with the full varint) allocation per event, 255 events per segment
  (`eventCount = msg.ReadByte()`), repeatedly => rapid OOM / GC claw / server hang. No `AllowFileTransfers`-like guard;
  works even for a client in the lobby, no game state needed.
- The skip path `msg.BitPosition += msgLength * 8` (line 503) is also unbounded (same as the previously reported
  NetPropertyData skip in ServerSettings.ServerRead) -> arbitrary jump/negative/overflow bit offset -> subsequent
  reads throw OutOfRange inside the `SegmentTableReader` callback; the outer catch in `ReadDataMessage` (via
  `DoSProtection`) keeps a client connected.
**MEDIUM — arbitrary-path filesystem probe via REQUEST_BACKUP_INDICES (GameServer.cs 1041-1051):**
- Any connected client sends `savePath` (arbitrary string); server calls `SaveUtil.GetIndexData(savePath)` which does
  `Directory.GetFiles(path, ".{fileName}.save.bak*")` (SaveUtil.cs 943-1021) and returns each backup's savetime,
  location, level type + echoes `savePath` back to the client. No permission/pawn/round guard. This is a blind
  directory probe / metadata disclosure of server filesystem (e.g. test for existence of arbitrary directories,
  read timestamps/level info from any `.save.bak*` matching a chosen baseName).
**LOW — OWNER client can force server shutdown via `ClientNetError` (GameServer.cs 1141-1149):**
- It's intentional (owner error ends the game), but a connected client whose chosen client name/spoofed connection
  equals OwnerConnection is treated as owner; no auth is re-verified here beyond the connection object set at
  startup. (Not a new bug; documented for completeness.)
MARKED ServerEntityEventManager 1-579, GameServer 824-1150, ServerSettings 120-637, CharacterNetworking 189-348.
[WAVE 64 — campaign ServerRead / store + fabricator]
**MEDIUM/LOW — MultiPlayerCampaign.ServerRead (server campaign 835-1159 + shared ReadPurchasedItems 294-315):**
- `ReadPurchasedItems` reads `UInt16 itemCount` (max 65535) per store (storeCount is byte, max 255) and loops
  `j < itemCount`, creating `List<PurchasedItem>` entries with no cap, then `CargoManager.PurchaseItems` iterates.
  A client near a store can push a large packet to allocate tens of thousands of objects per update → CPU/memory
  DoS. In practice after the wire bytes run out the read throws (caught by LidgrenServerPeer.Update); only if the
  attacker pads the packet does the allocation happen.
- `PurchasedItem.ItemPrefab => ItemPrefab.Prefabs[ItemPrefabIdentifier]` (CargoManager.cs 21) uses dictionary
  indexer. A client can send an arbitrary/nonexistent item identifier in the same campaign message; any use of
  `.ItemPrefab` (e.g. `GetBuyValuesAtCurrentLocation` CargoManager.cs 305, store stock find 1035) throws
  KeyNotFoundException. Since the whole inbound loop is wrapped in LidgrenServerPeer.Update try/catch, this is
  error/log spam, not a crash; still reachable with zero permissions from a client standing at a store.
- Store quantity is clamped by store stock/already-purchased (973-977, 1035-1036) and `TryPurchase` charges
  `item.Quantity * buyValue`; no free-item bug found in the campaign store path. `TryPurchase(price==0)` short-circuit
  is intentional for free (`price == 0`) services; not exploitable for normal goods (buyValue > 0).
- `Map.SetLocation(currentLocIndex)` at 941 only when `Map.AllowDebugTeleport` (debug); fine.
- Upgrades purchase gated by `HasCampaignInteractionAvailable(Upgrade)` (1127-1129) + UpgradeManager billing; map
  select gated by AllowedToManageCampaign(ManageMap) (946); crew/hire gated by HasCampaignInteractionAvailable(Crew)
  (1339/1376). No permission bypass seen here.
**LOW — Fabricator.ServerEventRead (server 9-32) uses `fabricationRecipes[recipeHash]` (indexer) directly; a
client with item access can send any non-existent recipeHash to throw KeyNotFoundException. Caught by
ServerEntityEventManager.Update (184-201), so it's analytics/log noise, not a crash. Same affect: `CircuitBox`
unknown opcode throws ArgumentOutOfRangeException (caught the same way).**
MARKED shared MultiPlayerCampaign 1-320, server MultiPlayerCampaign 1-250, GameServer 1740-1990.
[WAVE 65 — chat, rate limit, cargo confirmed]
- **ChatMessage.ServerRead (server 19-143):** text read then trimmed to MaxLength=200; rich-text `‖` stripped unless
  SpamImmunity; spam filter + per-client LastSentChatMessages. **NEW LOW:** `OrderChatMessage.ReadOrder` -> if the
  client sends an unknown `OrderIdentifier`, `OrderPrefab.Prefabs[...]` (server ChatMessage.cs:50) throws
  KeyNotFoundException. Caught by LidgrenServerPeer.Update; error noise, not a crash. (No permission check for
  issuing orders beyond having a live character + spam filter — gameplay-intended.)
- **RateLimiter.cs (1-134):** `IsLimitReached` is O(number of rate-limited clients) on every call and iterates the
  whole dictionary each packet; with many clients this is minor CPU churn. No bypass: exempt only owner/SpawnImmunity.
- **CargoManager.PurchaseItems** confirmed: price = quantity * `GetBuyValuesAtCurrentLocation`, `TryPurchase` charges
  before items are added; no free-item edge (already reported `price==0` short-circuit is for free services).
  `GetBuyCrateItem`/alreadyPurchased quantities capped; no unbounded quantity (max CargoManager.MaxQuantity).
MARKED shared ChatMessage 1-334, server ChatMessage 1-262, RateLimiter 1-134, shared CargoManager 1-60 & 265-345,

[WAVE 66 — EventManager conversation option OOB; file transfer info; upgrade/cargo]
**LOW/MEDIUM — EventManager.ServerRead (server EventManager.cs 28-102):** any connected client can send
`EVENTMANAGER_RESPONSE`. For an active ConversationAction targeted to the sender, `selectedOption` (byte) is used as
`convAction.Options[selectedOption]` (line 82) with **no bounds check** when the option isn't an ending option. If a
targeted client sends selectedOption >= Options.Count -> IndexOutOfRangeException inside the game loop (caught by
LidgrenServerPeer.Update, so error noise, not a crash). Non-targeted clients get `IgnoreClient` (correct).
**MEDIUM — campaign save disclosure via FILE_REQUEST (GameServer 1020-1024 + FileSender 399-408):** when
`AllowFileTransfers` is enabled (default), ANY connected client can request `FileTransferType.CampaignSave` and the
server sends the full current campaign save (character data, wallet/Bank balances, factions, upgrades, purchased
items). Intended for mid-round join sync, but there's no check that the client is not already in-game or that it
actually needs the save — a plain connected client can quietly dump the save. Combined with the `REQUEST_BACKUP_INDICES`
path probe from wave 63, the server leaks campaign state to any connection.
**UpgradeManager (shared 1-360):** purchase paths validate `HasPermissionToManageUpgrades(client)`, server-side
`price = prefab.Price.GetBuyPrice(...)`, `Campaign.TryPurchase(client, price)`; item swaps check `AvailableSwaps`,
same-category, swappability. `isNetworkMessage`/`force` only zero out the client-called price (client-side) — server
path always computes the real price. No bypass found.
**CargoManager/crew:** `ReadPurchasedItems` itemCount is the only unbounded loop (already logged); `CrewManager`
shared contains no client-writable protocol path.
MARKED shared NetEntityEventManager 1-107, shared KarmaManager 1-215, server EventManager 1-104, shared CrewManager
1-692, shared UpgradeManager 1-360.
[WAVE 67 — HIGH negative-transfer money printer; SegmentTable tablePointer]
**HIGH — negative TransferMoney amount = money creation (server Voting.cs 242-383 + Wallet.cs):**
- `ServerRead` for `VoteType.TransferMoney` reads `int amount` with **no sign/range check** and enqueues
  `TransferVote(sender, from, amount, to)` (line 324-332). `StartTransferVote` (GUI path) also has no check.
- `TransferVote.Finish` (lines 81-97) does `fromWallet.TryDeduct(TransferAmount)`. `Wallet.CanAfford(price) =>
  Balance >= price` and `Deduct(price) => Balance -= price` (Wallet.cs 185-205). With `price < 0`, `CanAfford` is
  always true and `Deduct(-N)` **increases** the payer's balance (clamped to `CampaignMode.MaxMoney`).
  Then `toWallet?.Give(negative)` only reduces the receiver (null-safe), so a client can repeatedly vote-transfer
  negative amounts from themselves (or the bank `from=null`) to create up to MaxMoney per vote.
- Activation requires the vote to actually run: `ServerRead` wrongly `enqueue`s directly instead of
  `StartOrEnqueueVote`, so if no vote is active the transfer sits in `pendingVotes`; an attacker can first start any
  other vote (e.g. Sub/SwitchSub) and then enqueue a transfer; when the first vote finishes, the transfer becomes
  Active. With `inGameClients.Count()==1` or a timeout+yes quorum it passes (Update line 213/228).
- Fix: reject `amount <= 0` and use `StartOrEnqueueVote`; ideally require `CanAfford` and a positive `amount`.
**LOW — SegmentTableReader.Read (SegmentTable.cs 265-324):** `tablePointer` and each segment's `Pointer` are
attacker-controlled and used as `msg.BitPosition = pointerLocation + pointer` with **no bounds check** before reading
the table. Malformed pointers read outside `LengthBits` but within the underlying buffer (stale/uninitialized bytes)
or throw OOR (caught by LidgrenServerPeer). This is the generic primitive that lets a client craft segment tables for
entity events (see the `ServerEntityEventManager.Read` huge-`msgLength` allocation in wave 63). By setting the table
to point inside its own payload, a client can drive `ReadBytes(msgLength)` allocations with attacker-picked lengths.
MARKED SegmentTable 1-330, server Voting 1-518, shared Wallet 1-237.
**Clarification:** the normal direct transfer packet IS guarded — `MultiPlayerCampaign.ServerReadMoney`
(1177-1233) rejects `Amount <= 0` and caps bank-request votes at `MaximumMoneyTransferRequest`. The vulnerable path
is only `Voting.ServerRead` (VoteType.TransferMoney), which reads the raw `int amount` and constructs `TransferVote`
without any sign/cap check. So a client can bypass the direct-transfer guard by using the vote message.
[WAVE 68 — BanList unbounded skip; name change; campaign save auto-send confirmed]
**LOW — BanList.ServerAdminRead non-Ban path (server BanList 341-348):** in `ServerSettings.ServerRead` (which
requires ManageSettings but not Ban) a client reads `removeCount = inc.ReadVariableUInt32()` and then
`incMsg.BitPosition += (int)removeCount * 4 * 8` — unbounded skip/overflow (same net-property-skip family). It can
push BitPosition arbitrarily (negative/overflow) and cause the subsequent read to throw (caught). Only semi-privileged
(ManageSettings) clients reach it.
**INFO — Campaign save auto-send (GameServer 2024-2045):** server auto-starts a CampaignSave `FileSender` transfer
to any client whose `LastRecvCampaignSave` is behind `campaign.LastSaveID`. That's the intended join-sync path and
confirms that `FILE_REQUEST`/auto-send means any connected client can pull the full campaign save without being
in-game (info leak already noted in wave 66/67).
**No bug in name change:** `ReadClientNameChange`/`TryChangeClientName` sanitize, validate name, check cooldown,
homoglyph collisions, owner exception. `ReadPermissions` is only used by a ManagePermissions admin; commandCount
unbounded but admin-only.
MARKED shared Client 234-266, server ChildServerRelay 1-31, server BanList 1-367, shared BanList 1-54,
GameServer 1997-2046 & 3458-3525.
[WAVE 69 — VOIP]
- `VoipQueue.Read` (shared 136-169): client controls 8 `bufferLengths` (byte, max 255) -> `msg.ReadBytes(len)`
  allocates up to 255 bytes each (<=2KB/voice update). Allocation happens before bounds check; if a client sends
  VOICE with len=255 but short payload, ReadBytes reads beyond `LengthBits` (within the backing array) and eventually
  throws OOR -> caught by peer, error noise. No OOM (bounded by 255).
- Discard path (`msg.BitPosition += len*8`, line 165) is the usual unbounded skip but len is a byte, so bounded.
- `VoipServer.Read` (132-147) does not rate-limit incoming VOICE beyond `DoSProtection`/`VoiceChatEnabled`; a client
  can flood ~2KB packets + 8 array allocations each -> moderate bandwidth/GC DoS. `ForceLocal` is client-controlled
  but only disables radio (cannot extend range). Muted senders are dropped at `CanReceive`.
MARKED shared VoipQueue 1-173, server VoipServer 1-148.
[WAVE 70 — Client/ServerLog confirmed]
- `Client.SetVote(voteType,value)` indexes `votes[(int)voteType]` with no bounds (shared Client 207-215). The
  network path `Voting.ServerRead` only calls SetVote for known enum values, but `VoteType` comes from a raw `byte`
  and unknown values fall through the switch. If any future/non-vanilla path passes an out-of-range VoteType, it
  throws OOR; no current remote trigger found.
- `votes` array size = `Enum.GetNames(typeof(VoteType)).Length`; `ResetVotes` uses `votes.Length` (safe).
- `Client.IsValidName` (server Client 186-214) validates disallowed chars + allowed ranges; name sanitize + max 32.
- `ServerLog` is server->file only; `WriteLine` bounded by `LinesPerFile`; no client path.
MARKED shared Client 1-291, server Client 1-362, shared ServerLog 1-188.

[WAVE 73 - Lidgren low-level: NEW HIGH - unprivileged memory-exhaustion + permanent leak via fragmentation]
Files read: NetPeer.Fragmentation.cs (1-169 FULL), NetFragmentationHelper.cs (1-175 FULL),
  NetBuffer.Read.cs (83-112, 554-587), NetPeer.Internal.cs (78-94, 457-595),
  NetConnection.cs (488-520), NetReliableOrderedReceiver.cs (FULL), NetUnreliableUnorderedReceiver.cs (FULL).
**FINDING A (HIGH) - HandleReleasedFragment (NetPeer.Fragmentation.cs 106-169):**
  Chain verified end-to-end: client Data packet with fragment bit -> NetPeer.Internal.ReceiveSocketData
  (472-590) sets msg.m_isFragment -> sender.ReceivedMessage -> channel receiver -> m_peer.ReleaseMessage
  (Internal.cs 78-94) -> HandleReleasedFragment.
  - Fragment header (NetFragmentationHelper.ReadHeader 53-105): group, totalBits, chunkByteSize,
    chunkNumber are CLIENT varints with NO caps (the `num2 & 0x1f` shift even wraps; asserts are
    [Conditional("DEBUG")] - compiled out in release).
  - totalBytes = BytesToHoldBits(totalBits); `info.Data = new byte[totalBytes]` (line ~138) -> allocation
    of up to ~256MB per unique group id, driven purely by the client, BEFORE any plausibility check
    (no relation to MTU/real transfer, no cap on chunkByteSize or totalNumChunks).
  - `m_receivedFragmentGroups` (Dictionary<NetConnection, Dictionary<int, ReceivedFragmentGroup>>) is
    NEVER evicted: removed only when a group completes (line 152); no timeout (LastReceived is commented
    out!), no per-connection limit, and the outer NetConnection key is retained => a client that sends
    N incomplete fragments with N unique group ids pins N * totalBytes bytes AND keeps its disconnected
    NetConnection alive (GC root). Verified by grep: no other writes to the dict anywhere.
  - Exceptions (BlockCopy overflow when payload > chunkByteSize, negative array size from negative
    totalBits) are caught per-packet in ReceiveSocketData (583: "Packet parsing error") => network thread
    survives; the memory impact remains.
  => NEW HIGH: any authenticated client on ANY dedicated (Lidgren/IP) server gets a controlled
     OOM/leak primitive at the transport layer - same family as Wave 50 MessageDefragmenter (which was
     P2P-only), but this one is the stock Lidgren path used by all IP servers. Not logged in any
     previous wave or in the user's analyses.
**FINDING B (LOW) - NetBuffer.ReadBytes(int) (NetBuffer.Read.cs 83-91):**
  `NetException.Assert` is [Conditional("DEBUG")] => in release, `new byte[numberOfBytes]` is allocated
  before any bounds check. Any library-level call path with a client-controlled count = allocation DoS
  primitive. Contrast: ReadString (554-587) DOES validate the varint length prefix against remaining
  bits before GetString (release returns null "to protect against DDOS") => strings are safe, raw
  ReadBytes(count) is not. Barotrauma's own Message wrapper has the same alloc-first pattern (Wave 63).

    [0, MaxStrength] with ranged 8-bit writes, limb index ranged. Server-derived only.

    fixed sizes; limb index ranged [0, limbHealths.Count-1]. Minor hostile-server note: the periodic

Lidgren closure (remaining transport files):
  - NetUnreliableSequencedReceiver (33) + NetReliableSequencedReceiver (65) FULL: window-bounded
    sequence logic, no allocation, no OOB. Clean.
  - NetConnection.MTU.cs (1-182 FULL): MTU expansion is INERT in Barotrauma - AutoExpandMTU defaults
    to false (NetPeerConfiguration.cs:121) and neither peer enables it (server explicitly sets
    AutoExpandMTU=false at LidgrenServerPeer.cs:29; client never touches it). Forged
    ExpandMTUSuccess is ignored on both sides (gate at NetConnection.cs:469). ExpandMTURequest is
    answered by SendMTUSuccess(payloadLength) - tiny echo, no state change. NO exploit.
  - NetReliableSenderChannel (60-115, 250-290) + GetResendDelay: reliable resends have NO max attempt
    count and NO exponential backoff (resend every 25ms + 2.1*RTT). Window = 64
    (NetConstants.ReliableOrderedWindowSize). A client that stays connected but withholds ACKs forces
    the server to re-send its 64-message window every ~resend delay until ConnectionTimeout
    (default 25s; 60s on lenient servers) drops it => bounded bandwidth amplification. LOW/INFO.
    Memory is bounded by the window (no growth).

  REWARD_DISTRIBUTION/RESET_REWARD_DISTRIBUTION (TabMenu/HRManagerUI), SERVER_SETTINGS(+PERKS),
